                       *****RULES FOR PUBLIC COMMENT*****

THE DELTA COUNTY BOARD OF COMMISSIONERS HAS ADOPTED THE FOLLOWING RULES:

  1. THE SPEAKER WILL BE LIMITED TO THREE (3) MINUTES, WITH NO EXCEPTIONS
  2. ONLY ONE PERSON IS ALLOWED AT THE PODIUM AT A TIME
  3. THE SPEAKER MUST IDENTIFY THEMSELVES BY NAME
  4. THE COMMENTS OF THE SPEAKER MUST RELATE TO BUSINESS THAT IS WITHIN THE
  JURISDICTION OF THE BOARD
  5. THE SPEAKER SHALL REFRAIN FROM MAKING PERSONAL ATTACKS UPON ANY
  INDIVIDUAL
  6. NO PROFANITY

  THE COUNTY OF DELTA WILL PROVIDE REASONABLE AUXILIARY AIDS AND SERVICES FOR
  THE HEARING IMPAIRED AND TO INDIVIDUALS WITH DISABILITIES AT THE
  MEETING/HEARING UPON REASONABLE NOTICE TO THE COUNTY OF DELTA. INDIVIDUALS
  WITH DISABILITIES REQUIRING SERVICES SHOULD CONTACT THE COUNTY OF DELTA ADA
  COORDINATOR BY WRITING OR CALLING THE FOLLOWING:

        1. New Deputy Records Clerk Position – Clerk Przewrocki introduced a job
           description for a new position in the Clerk’s office entitled, Deputy Records Clerk.
           She handed out reports of other Clerk/Register of Deeds Offices throughout the
           Upper Peninsula and the number of positions in each office. Handouts of statistics
           of revenue generated from the Clerk’s Office as well as workflow counts were also
           presented. Commissioner Johnson requested more information on part-time
           employee costs for Chippewa and Houghton counties. There was discussion
           regarding the current Clerk’s Office staffing numbers and a recommendation was
           given by Clerk Przewrocki to add a full-time position and eliminate either one or
           both part-time positions. A motion to defer this agenda item until more information
           is received, was made by Commissioner Johnson. Support by Commissioner van
           Ginhoven. Motion carried.

                              DELTA COUNTY, MICHIGAN
                               POSITION DESCRIPTION

         POSITION TITLE: EMERGENCY MANAGEMENT COORDINATOR

GENERAL SUMMARY:

The Emergency Management Coordinator reports to the Delta County Administrator and is
appointed by the Board of Commissioners consistent with the Michigan Emergency Management
Act (Act 390 of 1976) MCL 30.409(1).

The Emergency Management Coordinator with provide coordination services to Delta County.

The Emergency Management Coordinator shall be responsible for the coordination of all matters
pertaining to emergency management in the counties, including mitigation, preparedness,
response, and recovery.

ESSENTIAL FUNCTIONS:

   •   Responsible for adherence to the Michigan Emergency Management Act (Act 390 of
       1976)
   •   Coordinates emergency services, disaster response, and recovery assistance for Delta
       County.
   •   Manage and update County Emergency Action Guidelines to comply with best practices.
   •   Serves as liaison with the Michigan State Police Eighth District Coordinator, Emergency
       Management Division.
   •   Prepare, submit, and reconcile the annual emergency management budget.
   •   Required on call 24/7 in case of emergency.
   •   Ability to write grants and adhere to grant compliance standards.

MINIMUM QUALIFICATIONS:

   •   Associates Degree required; Bachelor’s Degree preferred. Higher Education field of
       study should be related to the position applied for.
   •   Two years of experience related to law enforcement or emergency response.
   •   Strong preference for applicants that have experience and knowledge related to current
       technology.

PHYSICAL REQUIREMENTS:

   •   Ability to enter and access information from a computer and smart phone.
   •   Ability to access office files.
   •   Ability to lift and move equipment weighing up to 45 lbs.
   •   Ability to conduct damage assessment which may require ground mobility for 5+ miles.
   •   Ability to sit at a computer for a prolonged period of time.


                                               1


COMPENSATION:

Wage dependent upon experience. Eligible for Delta County Health Insurance and Defined
Compensation.


Bd approved 8-16-22 to take effect on 10/1/22

Re:          Legal Representation

Dear Ms. DeSalvo:

This letter, along with the attached Additional Terms of Engagement, will serve as an agreement between
Foster, Swift, Collins & Smith, P.C. (the “Firm”) and Delta County (the “Client”) to provide legal services
in connection with employee benefits matters.

        A.      Representation. All legal work that the Firm performs pursuant to this letter is provided in
the Firm’s capacity as legal counsel to the Client. The Client agrees that the Firm does not, with regard
to any employee benefit plan, represent any retirement plan committee that has been or will be created by
the Client or the Company, any trustee for any employee benefit plan that is or will be maintained by the
Client or the Company, or any employee of the Client or Company. Advice that the Firm provides relating
to any of such third parties is provided to the Client only. The Client acknowledges and understands that
advice that the Firm provides to the Client regarding employee benefit plan fiduciary matters may not be
protected by the attorney-client privilege.

       B.     Cost. The Firm’s services will be billed on the basis of hourly rates for the time incurred.
The range of 2026 hourly rates for individuals expected to perform legal services for the Client is as
follows:


Delta County
August 27, 2026
Page 2

                A.      Shareholders                             $300.00         -       $600.00
                B.      Associates/Senior Attorneys              $240.00         -       $375.00
                C.      Legal Assistants                         $160.00         -       $250.00

                The hourly rates are set depending upon experience and expertise. The hourly rates are
subject to adjustment from time to time. The Client will also be billed for certain costs which the Firm
incurs on its behalf. Examples of such costs include certain photocopies and filing fees that the Firm pays
on behalf of the Client. The Firm will bill its costs and attorney fees monthly. Our invoices will be sent
by email, unless you direct us to send them in some other manner. Absent other agreed upon payment
terms, we will expect to receive payment within 30 days of the date of our invoice.

       C.     Additional Matters. The Firm believes that its representation of the Client complies with
the Michigan Rules of Professional Conduct. If we determine that a conflict of interest arises during this
engagement, the Firm may take appropriate steps to remedy the conflict, including withdrawal from the
engagement.

                Our responsibility in representing the Client is to do so in a manner that is consistent with
customary professional practices and requirements for handling matters in which we are involved. In turn,
we will need the Client’s full and timely cooperation. This will include providing us with various materials
relating to the matters for which the Client is retaining us.

               The Firm will pursue its work on behalf of the Client conscientiously and without delay,
but with regard for the Firm’s workload and the nature of the legal system. The Firm will keep the Client
reasonably informed about the status of the matters in which we become involved and welcomes requests
for information.

                We intend to continue a mutually rewarding and enduring relationship with the Client as
its legal counsel for this employee benefits work. Nevertheless, the Client is free to terminate our services
at any time by written notice to us to that effect. We may also terminate our services to the Client, by
written notice to the Client to that effect, if it unreasonably fails to cooperate with us, if it fails to pay our
monthly statements in a timely manner, or if we determine that our continued representation of the Client
would violate the rules of professional responsibility that apply to lawyers or would otherwise be
impractical.

               If, in the future, our work relationship with the Client ends, the Firm will return to the
Client any original materials in the Firm’s files that belong to the Client. The Firm will dispose of its files
(including the Firm’s work product) related to its employee benefits work for the Client as it sees fit.

                 Although this letter is intended to deal with the specific matters referenced above, it will
also govern any other legal services that we may render to you in the future, unless we mutually agree in
writing to a different arrangement with respect to these future matters. The terms of this letter are intended
to remain in force until changed by the Client and/or the Firm.


Delta County
August 27, 2026
Page 3

                Please closely review the Agreement to Arbitrate in the attached Additional Terms of
Engagement. By your signature below you are representing that, after being informed by independent
counsel or by your review of the attached arbitration provision and this letter, you understand that while
your ability to report unethical conduct is not restricted, by your agreement to arbitrate you are: waiving
the right to a jury trial, submitting to a private proceeding unlike litigation in court, potentially waiving
the right to take discovery to the extent available in court, waiving or limiting the right to appeal subject
to specific circumstances established by law, agreeing to be financially responsible for at least a share of
the arbitrator’s compensation and the administrative fees. You also understand and have considered
advantages to arbitration, where the parties can select an arbitrator who is experienced in the subject matter
of the dispute and arbitration may be less expensive, more efficient, and quicker than litigation in court.

Please do not hesitate to call us if you have questions regarding this letter. If you agree with the foregoing,
please sign this letter where indicated and return a copy to us by email. Your return of the signed letter is
the first step in our representation of the Client. We look forward to serving you.

Very truly yours,

FOSTER SWIFT COLLINS & SMITH PC

                                   Additional Terms of Engagement

Who we represent: We will represent only the person(s) or entity expressly named in the attached
engagement letter. We do not, for example, represent the individuals that are directors, shareholders, or
officers if our engagement letter identifies an entity as our client. In addition, the advice and
communications we render on behalf of our client (the named person(s) or entity) should not be
disseminated to or relied on by any other parties without our prior written consent.

What we require of you: You agree to fully, accurately and truthfully provide to us all facts and
documents that may be relevant to your matter(s), to inform us of developments relating to such matters,
to cooperate fully with us, and to do so in a timely manner. We will rely on the accuracy of the information
you provide, and your complete and timely response, when we perform our services. You also agree to
promptly inform us of any change in ownership, entity structure or employment status, so that we may
again assess any possible conflict of interest.

No Guaranteed Result: Although we may express our opinions or beliefs concerning your matter or
various courses of action and the results that we anticipate, any such statement made by any attorney or
employee of Foster, Swift, Collins & Smith, PC (the “Firm”) is only an expression of opinion, based on
information available to us at the time, and must not be construed by you as a promise or guarantee of any
particular result. You agree that we have made no promises or guarantees regarding the outcome of your
matter.

Hourly Rates: Effective January 2026, our hourly rates fall within these ranges: Attorneys $ 245.00 - $
650.00; Paralegals $ 130 - $ 310. Our rates are adjusted annually.

Retainer: Any retainer we have required, prior to beginning our work, or if requested and agreed to at
any time during our relationship, will be deposited in our Trust Account. Absent any provision to the
contrary in the attached engagement letter: we will draw from your retainer to pay fees and costs as they
are incurred, and our monthly invoice will reflect payments made in that manner; and if the balance
remaining in our Trust Account is insufficient to cover any invoice, you have agreed to pay the balance
due on the invoice, pay the amount needed to replenish the retainer, and to pay our monthly invoices while
we hold the retainer for payment of what we expect to be our last invoice for your matter. At the conclusion
of our engagement, after payment of all fees and costs that are due to us, we will return to you any
remaining retainer.

Vendors: We engage third party vendors to include but not be limited to providers of software,
applications, cloud storage, and generative AI. These vendors will have access to and may store
information that you have provided to us, documents that we have prepared for you, and communications
with you or made by us on your behalf. You agree to such access, but state that you do not intend to waive
any attorney-client privilege.

Our Use of Generative AI. We may make use of generative AI tools, which the Firm has vetted for
purposes to include assessing how the vendor handles any information that we provide when using the
tool and whether the tool produces accurate, verifiable results. We invite your inquiries regarding the
tools that we use and our vetting process. If you object in writing to our use of generative AI tools, we
will not use generative AI tools when providing services specific to your matter.


Your Use of Generative AI. Many generative AI tools do not produce accurate, reliable information or
results. Some results may appear impressive but are not accurate and, as a result, are highly misleading.
This problem, commonly referred to as “hallucinations,” has been particularly problematic in the legal
industry. Additionally, if something that you provided to us or something that we prepared and shared
with you is, without our prior advice and consent, uploaded by you into a generative AI tool or shared by
you with a vendor of generative AI services, you may be disclosing information that we would advise you
to keep confidential. Accordingly, you agree that you will not upload or otherwise submit for generative
AI review or share with any vendor of generative AI services any information, communication or
document pertinent to your matter or any writing that we prepared and shared with you, to avoid any
potential waiver of the attorney-client privilege or other harm to your interests or our relationship.

Electronic Communications: We will use email and other forms of electronic communication, which
may include text messages, unless you notify us in writing not to use a specified means of communication.
Unless you request and we agree, in writing, to encrypt our communications, documents sent to you by
electronic means may not be encrypted. We will use reasonable, generally acceptable protocols when
communicating with you and will expend reasonable efforts to exclude virus or other defects that might
affect our IT system and our communications. We do not accept liability for any loss or damage resulting
from the use of commercial software, the internet, or the receipt or use of electronic communications
containing a virus or other defect.

Payments, Charges and Liens: If not otherwise stated in the attached engagement letter, payment is due
within thirty (30) days of the date of our invoice. If an invoice is not timely paid, a late charge may be
added to any portion not paid within thirty (30) days. The late charge will be computed at the rate of .58%
per month (7% annual) starting thirty (30) days after the date of the invoice. All payments by way of
recovery, award, judgment, or settlement to you from third parties shall be made jointly payable to you
and the Firm. If you obtain a money judgment or award, you agree that we will have a lien on the proceeds
to recover fees and costs due the Firm.

Consultations: We represent many clients in a wide variety of matters, and during our representation of
you we may consult with the Firm's General Counsel regarding ethical issues. Additionally, we may
consult with persons outside the Firm who have expertise pertinent to your matter. While we strive to
conduct those consultations in a manner that will be protected by the attorney-client privilege, some courts
in some circumstances have ruled that such consultations may not be privileged. If we determine that it
is appropriate to conduct such a consultation: you agree that we may do so, and in the process share
information regarding our representation of you; but state that you do not intend to waive any attorney-
client privilege.

Corporate Transparency Act Reporting:
   Disclaimer. Unless specifically requested and agreed in writing, we will not: advise you regarding
   your obligation to submit or report information or update information already provided to any federal
   or state agency under the Corporate Transparency Act (31 U.S.C. § 5336) or comparable state law
   (“CTA Report”); assist you in preparing any CTA Report; directly file any CTA report on your behalf;
   or monitor any changes of circumstances (e.g., change in beneficial ownership, change of address)
   that may require you to submit or update a CTA Report.


   Right to Withdraw. Notwithstanding the previous paragraph, if we agree to assist you in preparing a
   CTA Report, you agree: (i) to fully cooperate in gathering and providing all information necessary for
   a CTA Report; and (ii) that we may withdraw from representing you under this agreement if you fail
   to timely provide all necessary information.
   Vendors. We partner with third party vendor(s) who, at your direction and expense, can store
   information necessary to prepare a report and submit a report on your behalf.
   Necessary Information. If a CTA Report requires information about an attorney or staff member of
   our Firm, we will cooperate in providing you the information necessary for that CTA Report.

Circular 230 Notices: Federal legislation imposes reporting requirements and penalties, which the IRS
has implemented by amending Circular 230 and setting forth various rules about written tax advice. As a
result, we may include a Circular 230 statement on written communications to you about reliance on the
advice that you receive.

Relationship Term/Other Representation: When we complete the scope of our work, our attorney-
client relationship shall end, regardless of the date you are billed or pay for our services. Any provision
of non-professional services (ie. file storage), and any newsletter, email, or other publication that we may
send to you addressing legal topics, do not revive an attorney-client relationship.
We are of the opinion that we have no obligation to represent you in related matters and no duty to accept
future engagements. Unless we have agreed to do so in writing, we have not undertaken any responsibility
to represent you or render advice in any matter other than the matter addressed in the attached engagement
letter. If you desire our representation in related or future matters, you agree to communicate your request
in writing. The terms stated above and in the attached engagement letter will govern any services that we
agree to provide in the future absent written agreement to different terms.

1         DELTA COUNTY GOVERNMENT

1.1       INTRODUCTION

This policy and procedure manual is being provided as a source of information for a variety of human resource issues,
programs, and policies. This manual is for informational purposes only and is not intended as an employment contract or
guarantee of benefits. Delta County Government reserves the right to alter, amend, modify, change, or delete any of
the policies, practices, or benefits described in this manual at any time, with supermajority Delta County Board of
Commissioners approval. When portions of this manual are revised or deleted, department heads and supervisors will
receive notification of the changes. In addition, notification of the changes will be made to the manual and posted on
the Delta County website. Once changes have been communicated, all employees must comply with the changes.

This policy manual provides a summary of the rules and regulations that govern the relationship between the Delta
County Board of Commissioners and employees, provides information on what is expected, guidance on how to resolve
any work-related problems that may arise, and set standards by which performance is evaluated. It is Delta County’s
belief that the standards follow the Delta County Mission Statement and that employees use the established vision
statements to guide their daily employment. Throughout this policy, the use of Delta County, Delta County Board of
Commissioners, County Board, Board, and Board of Commissioners, all refer to the Delta County Board of Commissioners
as the central governing body and policy creator.

Should you have any questions concerning any of the material in this manual, please contact Delta County Administration
Department.

1.2       WELCOME

Welcome to Delta County, Michigan Government. We are grateful for the opportunity to have you become a part of our
working team and hope that your time here will be rewarding for you, your family, and the citizens of Delta County. You
are now part of a team that is devoted to serving its citizens with transparency, efficiency, integrity and exemplary
customer service, as described in our adopted Mission Statement.


MISSION STATEMENT
Delta County Government serves the public in accordance with Michigan law by responsibly managing taxpayer resources
and providing transparent, accountable, and professional governance. We are committed to delivering specialized and
responsive public services; upholding the fair and impartial administration of justice through our courts; protecting public
safety; and stewarding the County’s natural resources to promote the health, welfare, and quality of life of our residents
now and in the future.

Vision Statements

      1. Transparent and Service-Oriented Government
         Delta County will operate as a transparent, ethical, and fiscally responsible government that places the needs of
         the public first, ensures accessibility to services, and fosters trust through open communication and accountable
         decision-making.
      2. Justice, Public Safety, and the Rule of Law
         Delta County will uphold the rule of law by supporting an independent and impartial court system and professional
         public safety services that protect individual rights, promote fairness, and ensure the safety and security of the
         community in accordance with Michigan statutes.
      3. Responsible Stewardship and Sustainability
         Delta County will responsibly manage and preserve its natural resources, public lands, and infrastructure through
         sound policy and long-term planning, ensuring environmental sustainability and economic vitality for current and
         future generations.



As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                        Page 6 of 55


1.3     APPLICABILITY OF POLICIES

PURPOSE

The purpose of this policy is to establish the scope, authority, and applicability of the Delta County Policy Manual
(hereafter referred to as the “Manual”). It ensures a consistent, equitable, and lawful application of personnel policies
across all County Departments and operations.

POLICY STATEMENT

The policies contained within this Manual serve as the primary governing framework for employment practices and
expectations within Delta County. Unless explicitly stated otherwise, these policies apply to all individuals who receive
compensation, in whole or in part, from County funds.

SCOPE OF APPLICABILITY

This Manual applies to all County personnel, categorized as follows:

        1.       Regular Full-Time and Part-Time Employees: All classified and unclassified staff members who have
                 completed their initial introductory period.
        2.       Introductory/Probationary Period: Employees currently serving their initial evaluation period, subject to
                 the limitations outlined in specific policies.
        3.       Temporary, Seasonal, and Per Diem Employees: Applicable only to the extent that specific policies (such
                 as Workplace Safety, Anti-Harassment, and Conduct) govern their daily employment. They are generally
                 excluded from County-sponsored benefit programs unless mandated by the State of Michigan or Federal
                 Law.
        4.       Appointed Officials and Department Heads: Applicable to all appointed leaders, except where specific
                 employment contracts or statutory provisions take precedence.

LIMITED APPLICABILITY AND SPECIAL GROUPS

Certain groups within the County structure have unique applications under this Manual:

        1.       Elected Officials: The personnel policies governing conduct, benefits, and administration do not dictate
                 the terms of office for Constitutional or Elected Officials. However, Elected Officials are strongly
                 encouraged to adopt these policies within their respective offices to ensure County-wide consistency.
        2.       Collective Bargaining Agreements (CBAs): For employees represented by a labor union, the terms of the
                 operative CBA shall take precedence over this Manual in the event of a direct conflict. Where the CBA is
                 silent, the policies in this Manual shall apply.

ADMINISTRATIVE AND AUTHORITY

The Board of Commissioners hold the ultimate authority to adopt, amend, rescind, or supplement any policy within this
Manual by a supermajority vote.

The Administration Department is responsible for the routine administration, interpretation, and enforcement of these
policies. Administration will conduct biennial reviews to ensure compliance with changing state and federal laws.

Department Heads and/or Elected Officials are responsible for enforcing these policies within their respective
departments uniformly and without discrimination.

POLICY CONFLICTS AND SEVERABILITY




As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                       Page 7 of 55


Individual departments may implement internal operational procedures to meet specific logistical needs. However,
departmental rules cannot be less restrictive or contradict the policies established in this Manual. In any conflict, this
Manual prevails.

If any provision of this Manual is declared invalid or unconstitutional by a court of competent jurisdiction, or through
changes in state/federal law, the remaining portions of the Manual shall remain in full force and effect.

EMPLOYEE ACKNOWLEDGEMENT

Every employee is required to read, understand, and comply with the provisions of this Manual. Upon hire, and following
any major revisions to the Manual, employees must sign an Acknowledgement of Receipt and Compliance Form, which
will be maintained in their permanent personnel file.

1.4     CONSTITUTIONAL OFFICERS, ELECTED OFFICIALS AND THEIR EMPLOYEES

PURPOSE

The purpose of this policy is to clarify the relationship between the Delta County Policy Manual (the “Manual”) and the
County’s Elected Officials and Constitutional Officers (collectively referred to as “Elected Officials”). This policy
recognizes the independent statutory authority granted to Elected Officials under state law while establishing a
framework for administrative consistency, risk mitigation, and compliance across all county operations.

STATUTORY AUTHORITY AND INDEPENDENCE

The County recognizes that Elected Officials (including but not limited to the Sheriff, County Clerk, County Treasurer,
Prosecutor, Drain Commissioner, County Surveyor, and Judges) possess independent statutory authority to manage the
internal operations of their respective offices, including the authority to hire, fire, and direct the daily activities of their
staff.

Consequently, the personnel and administrative policies established in the Manual do not automatically supersede the
constitutional or statutory authority of an Elected Official, except where mandated by federal or state law, or where
County-funded budgets and uniform benefit administration are concerned.

POLICY INTEGRATION AND OPTIONS FOR ELECTED OFFICIALS

To ensure legal protections and consistent treatment of all personnel compensated through County funds, Elected
Officials may govern their offices under one of the following frameworks:

        1.       Full Adoption (opt-in): The Elected Official may formally adopt the County Manual in its entirety to
                 govern all employees within their department.
        2.       Partial Adoption: The Elected Official may adopt specific chapters or sections of the County Manual while
                 drafting unique internal operational procedures or specialized personnel rules for areas unique to their
                 office’s function.
        3.       Independent Office Policy: The Elected Official may choose to operate under an independent personnel
                 policy manual tailored specifically to their office.

MANDATORY DEFAULT AND BASELINE RESTRICTIONS

If an Elected Official has not established an independent, legally sufficient personnel policy manual, or has not formally
notified Administration of a partial opt-in, their office and employees shall automatically default to the full coverage of
the County Manual.




As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                            Page 8 of 55


If an Elected Official implements an independent personnel policy, those policies cannot be less restrictive than the
standards established in the County Manual. Independent policies must meet or exceed County thresholds for professional
conduct, fiscal responsibility, and risk management.

Regardless of which option an Elected Official selects, certain core policies within the Manual are rooted in federal/state
statutory compliance or direct County liability and apply to all employees without exception. These include:

             •   Anti-Harassment, Discrimination, and Retaliation Policies
             •   Family and Medical Leave Act (FMLA) and Americans with Disabilities Act (ADA) Administration
             •   Drug-Free Workplace and Post-Accident Testing
             •   Workplace Safety (OSHA/Worker’s Compensation)
             •   County Information Technology and Network Security Protocols

FISCAL AND BENEFIT ADMINISTRATION

While Elected Officials retain personnel autonomy, the Delta County Board of Commissioners maintains statutory
authority over the County budget. Therefore:

        1.       All employees of Elected Officials are subject to the uniform classification scales, and benefits packages
                 established by the County or Collective Bargaining Agreement, unless alternative funding or structures
                 are explicitly authorized by state statute.
        2.       Independent office policies cannot alter County-funded compensation structures, overtime calculation
                 methods (unless required by law enforcement exemptions), or standard enrollment criteria for County
                 benefits.

NOTIFICATION OF POLICY ADOPTION

Elected Officials must provide written notice to the Administration Department within thirty (30) days of taking office,
or within thirty (30) days of the adoption of any County Manual updates, indicating whether they are opting into the
County Manual in full, in part, or operating under an independent policy.

If operating under an independent policy, a complete digital copy of that policy manual must be filed with the Ae
administration Department to ensure alignment with baseline County controls.

1.5     CODE OF ETHICS

PURPOSE

The citizens of Delta County are entitled to a county government that is fair, ethical, accountable, and transparent. The
purpose of this Code of Ethics is to establish clear guidelines for professional and personal conduct to ensure that all
individuals associated with the County maintain the highest standards of integrity, avoid conflicts of interest, and protect
the public trust.

APPLICABILITY

Unlike standard operational policies, this Code of Ethics applies universally and without exception. It governs all
individuals compensated by or representing Delta County, regardless of funding source, department, or civil service
status. This includes:

             •   All regular, temporary, seasonal, and part-time employees.
             •   All Constitutional Officers, Elected Officials, and Appointed Officials.
             •   All members of County Boards, Commissions, Authorities or Advisories.
             •   Volunteers and Independent Contractors while acting on behalf of the County.



As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                        Page 9 of 55


CORE STANDARDS OF ETHICAL CONDUCT

    1.       A Public Trust and Proper Use of Position
             a. Impartiality: Employees must perform their duties fairly and impartially. No employee shall grant any
                 special consideration, treatment, or advantage to any citizen, relative, or friend beyond that which is
                 available to every other citizen.
             b. Use of County Property: County-owned equipment, vehicles, materials, tools, and funds are strictly for
                 official County business. Personal use of County property, except for incidental communication
                 authorized under the County’s technology policy, is prohibited.
             c. Prestige of Office: No employee or official shall use the prestige or influence of their County position for
                 private gain, personal advancement, or the advancement of a personal associate.
    2.       Conflict of Interest
             a. Financial Interests: No employee shall participate in any official County decision, external negotiation,
                 or contract review in which they, a spouse, domestic partner, dependent child, business partner, close
                 personal friend, or recent employer has a direct or indirect financial interest.
             b. Disclosure: If a potential conflict or interest arises, the employee must immediately disclose the nature
                 of the conflict in writing to their Department Head and the Administration Department, and recuse
                 themselves from any further involvement in the matter.
             c. Outside Employment: Employees may engage in outside employment provided it does not create a
                 conflict of interest, interfere with the performance of their County duties, or occur during their
                 scheduled County work hours.
    3.       Gifts, Favors, and Gratuities
             a. General Prohibition: No employee shall solicit, accept, or agree to accept any gift, favor, loan, service,
                 or hospitality from any person, firm, or corporation that is currently doing business with, seeking to do
                 business with, or regulated by Delta County.
             b. De Minimis Exceptions: Token promotional items (pens, calendars, or note pads of nominal value) or
                 occasional meals provided during an informational seminar or professional conference may be accepted,
                 provided the value does not exceed $100.00 from a single source in a calendar year.
    4.       Confidentiality and Open Records
             a. Protected Information: Employees routinely have access to sensitive data, including medical records,
                 pending litigation strategies, collective bargaining positions, and private citizen data. No employee shall
                 disclose confidential information acquired during their official duties without legal authorization.
             b. Public Records: Conversely, employees shall not withhold information that is legally defined as a matter
                 of public record under state/federal freedom of information laws.

POLITICAL ACTIVITY

While the County respects and encourages the right of all employees to participate in the democratic process as private
citizens, the following restrictions apply to protect the non-partisan integrity of County operations:

             •   Employees shall not engage in political campaign activities (including wearing campaign buttons,
                 distributing literature, or soliciting donations) during their designated County working hours or while
                 wearing a County uniform.
             •   No County funds, property, or equipment shall be used to support or oppose any political candidate or
                 ballot initiative.
             •   No supervisor shall coerce, pressure, or require any subordinate employee to contribute to a political
                 cause or participate in political activity.

REPORTING AND WHISTELBLOWER PROTECTIONS

Any employee who observes or has credible knowledge of a violation of this Code of Ethics has an affirmative duty to
report the matter immediately to their Supervisor, Department Head, or the Administration Department.



As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                        Page 10 of 55


Delta County is committed to protecting employees who report ethical violations in good faith. Retaliation, harassment,
or adverse employment action taken against any employee for reporting a suspected violation or participating in an
ethical investigation is strictly prohibited and constitutes a terminable offense.

Violations of this Code of Ethics compromise the integrity of the County and will not be tolerated. Following a thorough
investigation, any employee found to have violated this policy will be subject to disciplinary action up to and including
immediate termination of employment.

In cases where the ethical violation also constitutes a breach of state or federal criminal law (bribery, fraud, or
embezzlement), the County will refer the matter to the appropriate law enforcement authorities for prosecution.

1.6     OPEN DOOR POLICY

PURPOSE

Delta County is committed to providing a transparent, collaborative, and professional work environment where
communication flows freely. The purpose of this Open Door Policy is to encourage employees to voice their ideas, ask
questions, raise concerns, or discuss workplace issues directly with county management without fear of retaliation.

By addressing concerns early, equitably, and informally, the County aims to foster mutual trust and resolve workplace
challenges before they escalate.

APPLICABILITY

This policy applies universally to all county personnel across all departments, agencies, and offices. This includes full-
time, part-time, temporary, and seasonal employees, regardless of their position or length of service.

CORE PRINCIPLES

        1.       Accessibility: Every supervisor, department head, and Human Resources representative maintains an
                 “open door”. Employees are not restricted to speaking only with their immediate supervisor if they feel
                 a situation requires a different perspective.
        2.       Constructive Communication: The County encourages honest, professional, and respectful dialogue.
                 Employees may use the open-door framework to suggest operational improvements, discuss career
                 development, or resolve interpersonal workplace friction.
        3.       Informal Resolution First: While formal grievance procedures exist for specific contractual or legal
                 disputes, the Open Door Policy is intended to resolve day-to-day misunderstandings and operational
                 challenges informally and efficiently.

RECOMMENDED COMMUNICATION PROCEDURE

To ensure that issues are handled effectively, employees are encouraged to utilize the following steps, though they may
bypass steps if the nature of the issue makes doing so necessary:

        1.       Step 1: Immediate Supervisor
                 a. The immediate supervisor is usually closest to the day-to-day operations and is often in the best
                     position to quickly resolve misunderstandings or logistical issues.
        2.       Step 2: Department Head/Elected Official
                 a. If the issue involves the immediate supervisor, remains unresolved after Step 1, or is of a sensitive
                     operational nature, the employee is encouraged to schedule a meeting with their Department Head
                     or Elected Official.
        3.       Step 3: Administration




As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                       Page 11 of 55


                 a. If an employee feels uncomfortable discussing the matter within their own department, or if the
                    previous steps have not yielded a satisfactory resolution, the employee should contact the
                    Administration Department. Administration acts as an objective, neutral resource to facilitate
                    discussions and provide guidance.

CONFIDENTIALITY AND LIMITATIONS

The County will respect employee privacy and handle discussions under this policy with the utmost discretion. Information
shared during an open-door discussion will be kept confidential to the extent practical and legal.

Employees must understand that absolute confidentiality cannot be guaranteed if the discussion reveals information that
requires mandatory county action. If a conversation uncovers allegations of illegal activity, fraud, safety hazards,
discrimination, or harassment, management and Administration are legally obligated to investigate and take corrective
action.

STRICT PROHIBITION AGAINST RETALIATION

The success of an Open Door Policy depends entirely on employees feeling safe to speak up. Delta County strictly prohibits
any form of retaliation, harassment, or adverse employment action against an employee for utilizing this policy in good
faith, or for participating in an open-door discussion.

Any supervisor or department head who penalizes, penalizes through scheduling, isolates, or otherwise retaliates against
an employee for exercising their rights under this policy will be subject to immediate disciplinary action, up to and
including termination of employment.

1.7     EMERGENCY PROCEDURES/BUILDING EVACUATION/THREAT RESPONSE

PURPOSE

The purpose of this policy is to establish unified emergency response procedures across all Delta County facilities. It
outlines protocols for hazardous weather, physical building evacuations, employee accountability, and active threats.

ROLE OF EMERGENCY MANAGEMENT COORDINATOR

The County Emergency Management Coordinator (EM) serves as the central authority for comprehensive emergency
planning, risk assessment, and inter-agency coordination.

             •   Planning and Preparedness: The EM maintains the County Emergency Operations Plan (EOP), coordinates
                 facility vulnerability assessments, and schedules mandatory annual emergency drills (evacuations,
                 weather shelter, and threat response).
             •   Incident Command Integration: During a major facility incident, the EM liaisons between County
                 Administration, County Board Chair, local law enforcement, fire departments, and Emergency Medical
                 Services.
             •   Facility Safety Coordinator: The EM works with the County Administration to appoint and train a Facility
                 Safety Coordinator for each County facility to manage localized evacuations and accountability rosters.

Severe Weather and Building Shelter Protocols

When severe weather threatens County facilities, the following shall take place:

             •   Weather Watch: Operations continue, but Department Heads and Facility Safety Coordinators monitor
                 Emergency Alert System/WEA broadcasts and prepare designated interior shelter spaces.




As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                       Page 12 of 55


             •   Weather Warning: All employees, visitors, and members of the public inside County buildings must
                 immediately move to designated severe weather shelter areas (interior hallways, lower levels,
                 windowless rooms).
             •   Window Glass Hazard: Stay clear of exterior windows, glass skylights, and large open-span areas.
             •   All-Clear: Employees must remain in shelter locations until the official authorized first response
                 department issues a formal “All Clear”.

Facility Evacuation and Employee Accountability

In the event of a fire, gas leak, structural compromise, or hazardous material release, immediate building evacuation is
required.

             •   Evacuation Protocol: Activate Alarm-anyone observing smoke, fire, or severe hazard must immediately
                 pull the nearest fire alarm pull station and call 911. Orderly Exit-employees must cease work, secure
                 sensitive/confidential documents (if safe to do so), and proceed calmly to the nearest emergency exit
                 using stairs. Assist Visitors-employees are responsible for guiding members of the public in their
                 immediate work areas to emergency exits.
             •   Accountability and Head Count: Assembly Points-each County facility has a pre-designated Exterior
                 Assembly Point located a safe distance from the building. Roster Roll Call-Department Heads (or Facility
                 Safety Coordinator) must take their physical or digital emergency rosters to the Assembly Point and
                 perform an immediate headcount of all active staff scheduled that day. Missing Persons Reporting-
                 Facility Safety Coordinator must immediately report any missing or unaccounted for employee/visitor to
                 the EM or emergency responders on scene. No one may re-enter a building until authorized by the EM or
                 Fire Department.

Active Threat and Critical Incident Procedures (Standard Response Protocol)

Delta County has formally adopted the Standard Response Protocol (SRP) created by the “I Love You Guys” Foundation
and endorsed by the State of Michigan. The SRP relies on five clear, standardized action commands:

    1.       HOLD – Clear hallways and remain in offices/workspaces until the situation resolves. Business continues
             inside rooms with doors closed and locked.
    2.       SECURE – Threat is outside the facility (i.e. police activity nearby). Exterior doors are locked; access is
             controlled. Increased situational awareness, but normal interior operations continue.
    3.       LOCKDOWN – Threat is inside or attempting access to the facility. Lock office doors, turn off lights, move
             out of sight from windows/doors, remain silent, and prepare to defend if necessary. Do NOT open doors for
             anyone; law enforcement will unlock them.
    4.       EVACUATE – Used to move employees/visitors from one area to another. Follow instructions from EM/First
             Responders. Leave personal belongings behind, keep hands visible and empty.
    5.       SHELTER – Used for severe weather, hazardous materials, or environmental threats. Follow specific safety
             strategies for Severe Weather and Building Shelter Protocols above.

Active Shooter/Hostile Threat Response Strategy

If an active shooter breaches a building before a Lockdown can be called, or during a Lockdown breach, employees are
to employ the Run, Hide, Fight decision framework.

             •   RUN (Evacuate): If an accessible escape path exists, attempt to evacuate the premises immediately.
                 Leave belongings behind, keep hands visible for arriving law enforcement, and call 911 when it is safe.
             •   HIDE (Lockdown): If evacuation is impossible, lock and barricade doors with heavy furniture, turn off
                 lights, silence call phones (including vibration), hide behind large items, and stay low to the floor.




As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                      Page 13 of 55


             •   FIGHT (Counter): As a last resort, and only when your life is in imminent danger, act aggressively against
                 the shooter. Throw items, improvise weapons (fire extinguishers, heavy objects), yell, and commit fully
                 to incapacitating the threat.

Training and Maintenance

Drills: All County staff must participate in mandatory annual drills directed by the EM, including SRP Lockdown reviews,
fire evacuation drills, and severe weather shelter exercises.

New Hire Orientation: County Administration and EM will provide all new personnel with SRP reference materials and
facility safety maps upon hire.

1.8     HAZARDOUS WEATHER

PURPOSE

The purpose of this policy is to establish a clear, structured framework for operations during severe weather or other
emergency conditions. The primary goal of Delta County during any hazardous event is to ensure the safety of county
employees and the public, while maintaining essential public services to the greatest extent possible.

APPLICABILITY

This policy applies to all county employees. However, for operational purposes during an emergency, personnel are
divided into two distinct categories:

             •   Essential Personnel: Employees whose duties are critical to maintaining the continuity of public safety,
                 infrastructure, and emergency operations. This includes, but is not limited to, Sheriff’s Road Patrol and
                 Corrections, Emergency Management Staff, Dispatch Personnel, and Building and Maintenance Personnel.
             •   Non-Essential Personnel: Employees whose duties, while vital to daily county operations, can be delayed
                 or performed remotely without immediately jeopardizing public health or safety.

DECISION-MAKING PROCESS

The authority to close county facilities, delay operations, or implement early release rests solely with the County
Administrator with County Board Chair approval. The County Administrator is considered the “Deciding Authority”.

The decision-making process will rely on an assessment of local conditions, beginning as early as possible (ideally by
5:30am for morning closures). The Deciding Authority will consult with:

        1.       The County Road Commission and local Law Enforcement regarding road conditions and travel safety.
        2.       The National Weather Service regarding the timing, severity, and duration of the weather event.
        3.       Surrounding jurisdictions and local school districts to evaluate regional impact.

OPERATIONAL SCENARIOUS AND EMPLOYEE COMPENSATION

Depending on the severity of the weather, one of the following operational statuses will be declared:

        1.       Delayed Opening: Non-Essential staff report to work at the designated delayed time if safe to do so.
                 Essential staff report at regularly scheduled time. Regular pay for hours the county was closed.
        2.       Early Release: Non-Essential staff depart facilities at the designated closure time. Essential employees
                 remain on duty until properly relieved. Regular pay for the remainder of the scheduled shift.
        3.       Full Facility Closure: Non-Essential staff do not report to county buildings and work remotely if
                 authorized. Essential staff report to work at regularly scheduled time. Non-Essential staff receive regular
                 base pay. Essential staff are paid according to standard/overtime policy.



As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                        Page 14 of 55


        4.       County Open/Hazardous Travel: Non-Essential staff are expected to report but use personal discretion
                 regarding safety. Essential staff must report. Employees who choose to stay home must utilize benefit
                 time.

COMMUNICATION PROTOCOL TO EMPLOYEES

Once a decision regarding a closure, delay, or early release is finalized, notification will be disseminated immediately
using a multi-channel approach to ensure all staff are reached.

             •   Internal Emergency Alert System: A text message will be broadcast to all department heads and elected
                 officials with the decision/information and instructions to alert their employees.
             •   County Email System: A formal email blast detailing the closure and operational expectations will be
                 sent to all active employee accounts.
             •   Public and Media Notifications: Information will be posted on the official Delta County website and
                 departmental social media accounts, and broadcast on local television and radio stations.

EMPLOYEE RESPONSIBILITIES

It is the sole responsibility of every employee to ensure their current mobile number and emergency contact information
are kept up to date within the payroll portal. In an employee is in doubt regarding facility status, they should check the
county website or contact their immediate supervisor before commuting.

REMOTE WORK CAPABILITIES

If the County declares a facility closure but infrastructure (such as power and internet) remains functional, non-essential
employees who possess approved remote-work capabilities, are allowed to perform their standard job duties from home
during regular business hours. Remote work during an emergency closure does not alter the employee’s base
compensation.

2       HUMAN RESOURCES



2.1     CLASSIFICATION OF EMPLOYEES

PURPOSE

The Purpose of this policy is to establish clear definitions for employee classifications within Delta County, ensuring
compliance with the federal Fair Labor Standards Act (FLSA), the Internal Revenue Code, and applicable State of Michigan
labor statutes (including the Michigan Payment of Wages and Fringe Benefits Act, Act 390 of 1978, and the Michigan
Earned Sick Time Act).

Accurate classification determines eligibility for county-sponsored benefits, overtime compensation, retirement
enrollment, and statutory leave accruals.

FAIR LABOR STANDARDS ACT (FLSA) DESIGNATIONS

Regardless of employment category, every position at Delta County is designated as either Exempt or Non-Exempt under
the FLSA.

             •   Exempt Personnel: Employees whose specific primary job duties and salary level meet the exemption
                 criteria established by the FLSA (executive, administrative, professional, or specific computer-related
                 positions). Exempt employees are paid an established salary to perform a job function and are exempt
                 from receiving overtime pay.



As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                       Page 15 of 55


             •   Non-Exempt Personnel: Employees whose position does not meet FLSA exemption criteria. Non-exempt
                 employees are paid an hourly wage and are entitled to overtime pay (or compensatory time, where
                 applicable and legally authorized) at a rate of one and one-half (1.5X) times their regular rate of pay for
                 all hours worked beyond 40 hours in a single workweek (or applicable statutory thresholds for law
                 enforcement personnel under Section 207(k)).

EMPLOYMENT CLASSIFICATIONS

All personnel employed by Delta County fall into one of the following primary classifications:

    1. Regular Full-Time Employees
           a. Definition: an employee hired into an established or newly created continuing county position, scheduled
               to work a standard workweek of at least 35 to 40 hours per week (minimum 1,820 to 2,080 hours annually)
               on a year-round basis.
           b. ACA & Benefits: Classified as full-time under the Affordable Care Act (ACA) threshold (30+ hours/week
               or 130 hours/month). Regular full-time employees are eligible for the full suite of county-sponsored
               fringe benefits, including health insurance, paid time off, life insurance, and enrollment in the
               retirement system, subject to plan vesting and waiting periods.
    2. Regular Part-Time Employees
           a. Definition: An employee hired into an established or newly created county position scheduled to work on
               a regular, year-round basis, but whose normal schedule is fewer than 30 hours per week (or under 130
               hours per month).
           b. ACA & Benefits: Non-full-time under ACA standard definitions, and regular part-time employees are
               generally ineligible for standard county health insurance benefits unless mandated by specific plan
               documents or collective bargaining agreements.
           c. Michigan Earned Sick Time Compliance: Under Michigan Law, regular part-time employees accrue paid
               sick leave at a rate of at least 1 hour of paid sick time for every 30 hours worked, up to statutory limits,
               unless governed by an equal or more generous benefit time policy.
    3. Seasonal Employees
           a. Definition: An employee hired into a position created to perform duties for a temporary, limited duration
               tied to a specific season or recurring timeframe. The period of employment typically does not exceed
               120 days, or 6 consecutive months, in a calendar year. The seasonal timeframe is established by
               Administration and submitted to the Michigan Unemployment Insurance Agency annually.
           b. Legal & Benefits: Under Michigan Unemployment Insurance Agency (IUA) guidelines and federal
               standards, seasonal employees are informed upon hire of the temporary nature of the work. They are
               ineligible for county medical benefits, holiday pay, or retirement contributions, but accrue Michigan
               statutory paid sick time based on actual hours worked. Rehire for subsequent seasons does not grant
               regular full-time or part-time status.
    4. Temporary/Per Diem Personnel
           a. Definition: An employee hired to address short-term operational spikes, special projects, or temporary
               vacancies
    5. Contracted Personnel and Independent Contractors
           a. To maintain a strict compliance with IRS criteria, Michigan Department of Labor standards, and County
               procurement rules, a clear distinction is made between County Employees and Independent Contractors.
           b. Definition: Independent contractors (or 1099 vendors) are not county employees. They are self-employed
               individuals or third-party corporate entities engaged through a formal written contract to deliver a
               specific result or service.
           c. Statutory Standards: Under IRS behavioral, financial, and relational control factors, the County controls
               only the end result of the work, not the specific methods or manner of performance. Contractors
               maintain an independent business, invoice the County for services, utilize their own tools and equipment,
               and bear independent financial risk.



As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                        Page 16 of 55


           d. Restrictions: Independent contractors do not receive employee benefits, are not covered under County
               Workers’ Compensation or Unemployment Insurance, and cannot be used to circumvent established
               County personnel classifications. All independent contractor engagements must be vetted through the
               Administration Department and approved by Board of Commissioners before work commences.
    6. Reclassification and Status Review
           a. An position’s classification cannot be changed informally or by verbal agreement. Any reclassification
               (i.e. moving from part-time to full-time status) requires:
                     i. Job Description and job duty approval by the Personnel Committee.
                    ii. Budgetary approval by the Finance Committee.
                   iii. Board of Commissioners final approval.
                   iv. Written notification signed by the employee in the changed position, and placed in their official
                        personnel file.

ELECTED OFFICIALS AND APPOINTED POSITIONS

Under Michigan law and County governance, a fundamental distinction exists between elected officials and appointed
positions based on their source of authority and employment status.

    1. Elected Officials: Individuals chosen directly by the electorate or constitutional mandate. Elected officials hold
       public office by statutory or constitutional right for a fixed term. They are not “employees” in the traditional
       sense due to their term of office being fixed by law with some statutory requirements on their salaries. They
       cannot be hired, disciplined, or terminated through standard administrative HR procedures, and their primary
       duty is directly to the public.
    2. Appointed and Hired Positions: Individuals selected by the County Board, an Elected Official, or the County
       Administrator as the governing official. Appointed positions derive their authority from delegation rather than
       direct election. Depending on the position and governing statute, appointed officials may serve under a formal
       employment contract for a specified term or at the pleasure of the appointing body (at will), and their positions
       remain subject to the administrative, performance, and operational framework established in the County Manual.
       Hired positions derive their authority under collective bargaining unit contracts, with positions authorized to be
       filled, when vacant, under the County Board. These positions remain subject to the administrative, performance,
       and operational framework of the County Manual and their collective bargaining agreement as supervised by the
       corresponding Department Head.

DELTA COUNTY ORGANIZATIONAL CHART

Delta County strives to establish a structure based on a balance between elected independence, administrative
management, and citizen advisory input.

    1. Board of Commissioners – At the top of the structure is the Board of Commissioners, which possesses two distinct
       operational relationships across the organization:
           a. Financial Oversight (dotted lines) – The County Board exercises financial oversight over all
               constitutionally and statutorily elected/judicial positions. This includes the judges of the Circuit,
               District, and Probate Court, and Elected Department Heads such as the County Clerk/Register of Deeds,
               Sheriff, County Treasurer, Drain Commissioner, and Prosecuting Attorney.
           b. Full Oversight (solid lines) – The County Board exercises full oversight, both operationally and financially,
               over the County Administrator. The County Administrator manages all non-elected operational
               departments. The Administrator directs personnel, operational procedures, and day-to-day
               administration for these departments on behalf of the County Board.
    2. Advisory Board Definition and Roles – Advisory boards are citizen and stakeholder bodies established by statute
       or County resolution. Advisory Boards do not possess independent administrative power, nor do they directly
       employ staff or allocate County funds. Their primary role is to bring subject-matter expertise and community
       representation to the Department Head and County Administrator. Department Heads report directly to the



As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                       Page 17 of 55


        County Administrator, who synthesizes advisory recommendations before presenting policy or budgetary
        proposals to the Board of Commissioners for final approval. On the Delta County Organizational Chart (below),
        Advisory Boards appear under specific operational departments:
            a. Veterans Service Board – advises the Veteran Service Affairs department on County veteran affairs, relief
                funds, and local benefit programs.
            b. Airport Advisory Board – provides operational recommendations, facility development input, and policy
                guidance to the Airport Department and Airport Manager.
            c. 911 Advisory Board – assists the Dispatch Department by coordinating inter-agency emergency
                communications policy, dispatch procedures, and public safety technology needs among local fire,
                police, and EMS agencies.
            d. Parks Commission – advises the Parks and Recreation Department regarding county park maintenance,
                facility improvements, recreational grants, and park land management.

3.4       EQUAL OPPORTUNITY EMPLOYMENT NON-DISCRIMINATION

PURPOSE

Delta County is committed to maintain a workplace environment that values diversity, fosters respect, and protects the
civil rights of all employees and applicants. The purpose of this policy is to establish that Delta County is an Equal
Opportunity Employer (EOE) and to enforce a zero-tolerance standard against discrimination, harassment, and retaliation
in all aspects of personnel administration.

APPLICABILITY

This policy applies universally to all individuals seeking employment with, employed by, or representing Delta County.
This includes:

      •   All regular full-time, regular part-time, seasonal, temporary, and per diem employees across all departments.
      •   All employees, appointed staff, and personnel serving under Elected Officials.
      •   Applicants for employment, interns, volunteers, and independent contractors while conducting County business.

POLICY STATEMENT AND PROTECTED CLASSES

Delta County provides equal employment opportunities to all employees and applicants without regard to any status
protected by federal, state, or local law. Decisions regarding recruitment, hiring, placement, promotion, transfer,
training, compensation, benefits, discipline, and termination will be made strictly on the basis of merit, qualifications,
operational needs, and job performance.

      A. Federal Statutory Protections




As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                       Page 19 of 55


         Pursuant to Title VII of the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Americans with Disabilities Act (ADA), the Age
         Discrimination in Employment Act (ADEA), and the Pregnancy Discrimination Act (PDA), discrimination is
         prohibited based on:
             • Race, Color, or National Origin
             • Religion or Creed
             • Sex, Gender, Pregnancy, Childbirth, or Related Medical Conditions
             • Age
             • Physical or Mental Disability
             • Military or Veteran Status

    B. State of Michigan Statutory Protections
       In compliance with Michigan law, specifically the Elliott-Larsen Civil Rights Act and the Persons with Disabilities
       Civil Rights Act, Delta County, except where statutorily exempt, provides expanded protections prohibiting
       discrimination based on:
           • Height and Weight
           • Marital Status or Familial Status
           • Sexual Orientation and Gender Identity or Expression
           • Age

REASONABLE ACCOMODATIONS

Delta County will provide reasonable accommodations to qualified individuals with known physical or mental disabilities,
unless doing so creates an undue hardship on the operation of County services.

Under the Michigan Persons with Disabilities Civil Rights Act, an employee or applicant who requires an accommodation
to perform their job duties must notify the Delta County Administration Department in writing of the need for
accommodation within 182 calendar days after the date the employee knew, or reasonably should have known, that an
accommodation was needed.

The County will also provide reasonable accommodations for sincere religious observances, practices, or beliefs, of
employees and applicants, provided such accommodations do not impose an undue hardship on County operations or
compromise safety.

COMPLAINT AND REPORT PROCEDURE

Any employee, job applicant, or member of the public who believes they have been subjected to discrimination, unequal
treatment, or harassment in violation of this policy is strongly encouraged to report the incident immediately.

Reporting Options - Reports may be submitted in writing to:

    1.   The employee’s immediate supervisor or Department Head
    2.   Delta County Administration Department
    3.   Delta County Administrator
    4.   Delta County Board Chair
    5.   Delta County Board of Commissioners

If the complaint involves the employee’s direct supervisor or Department Head, the employee may report directly to the
Administration Department or County Administrator.

Upon receiving a complaint, the County will conduct a prompt, thorough, and objective investigation. Confidentiality
will be maintained to the greatest extent possible, consistent with a thorough review and applicable public record
disclosure laws.



As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                       Page 20 of 55


STRICT PROHIBITION AGAINST RETALIATION

State and Federal law, as well as Delta County policy, strictly forbid retaliation. No officer, manager, supervisor, or
employee shall discharge, threaten, harass, intimidate, or take adverse employment action against any individual
because they:

      •   Filed a good-faith report or complaint of discrimination under this policy.
      •   Participated as a witness or provided information in an internal or external investigation.
      •   Opposed an unlawful employment practice.

Any employee found to have engaged in retaliatory behavior will be subject to immediate disciplinary action, up to and
including termination of employment.

5.4     HOLIDAY LEAVE



5.5     SICK LEAVE



5.6     VACATION/PERSONAL/FLOATING HOLIDAY LEAVE

PURPOSE

Delta County provides benefit time to support the health, well-being, and work-life balance of its employees. The purpose
of this policy is to establish standard guidelines for the accrual, usage, rollover, and separation payout of Vacation Time,
Personal Days, and Floating Holidays for county personnel.

APPLICABILITY

This policy applies to all non-union/non-elected employees, except where stated otherwise and where employees are to
follow their collective bargaining agreements.




As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                        Page 22 of 55


    •   Bargaining Unit Employees – personnel governed by a Collective Bargaining Agreement (CBA) will follow the
        specific leave provisions outlined in their respective contract.
    •   Transfers – Union employees transitioning into a non-union/non-elected position will transfer over all existing
        benefit time, but will begin accruing at the new specified rate as of their first day of service in their new position.
        Non-Union/Non-Elected employees transitioning into a union position will transfer over all existing benefit time,
        but will begin accruing at the new specified rate as of their first day of service in their new position. In each
        scenario, the employee’s anniversary date will become their new date of hire in their new position, with their
        start level as a new employee.

VACATION LEAVE

Accrual Schedule

    •   Vacation time is granted based on completed years of continuous service according to the following schedule:
               Years of Continuous Service                    Annual Vacation Entitlement
               Hire through Year 2                            5 days
               Years 3 through 5                              10 days
               Years 6 through 9                              15 days
               Years 10 through 14                            20 days
               Years 15+                                      25 days (maximum)

    •   Newly appointed Department Heads begin accruals at Year 3 of the scale (10 days) upon hire.

VACATION ROLLOVER POLICY

Employees are encouraged to utilize their vacation time within the anniversary year in which it is earned.

    •   Rollover Maximum – a maximum of 24 hours of unused vacation time may be rolled over into the employee’s new
        anniversary year.
    •   Usage Window – all rolled-over vacation hours must be fully used within the first quarter of the employee’s new
        anniversary year. Any rolled-over hours remaining unused at the conclusion of the first quarter will be forfeited.
    •   Written Request Deadline – to request a rollover of unused vacation time up to the 24-hour maximum, the
        employee must submit a formal written request via email to County Administration at admin@deltacountymi.gov.
    •   Submission Window – the email request must be submitted prior to the employee’s anniversary date, but no
        sooner than 30 days prior to that anniversary date. No exceptions will be granted for late or non-conforming
        submissions.

PERSONAL DAYS AND FLOATING HOLIDAYS

Personal Days

    •   Entitlement – employees receive three (3) Personal Days available for use during each anniversary year.
    •   Interaction with Sick Leave – One (1) of the three (3) personal days, if used, is deducted from the employee’s
        accumulated sick leave balance.
    •   No Rollover – unused personal days cannot be rolled over into a subsequent anniversary year and are forfeited if
        unused.

Floating Holidays

    •   Entitlement – employees receive four (4) Floating Holidays per year in addition to the ten (10) designated County
        holidays.




As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                           Page 23 of 55


      •   Scheduling – floating holidays must be scheduled in advance with departmental approval. Unused floating holidays
          do not roll over from year to year.

SEPARATION OF EMPLOYMENT AND PAYOUTS

Upon termination, retirement, or voluntary separation from Delta County service:

      •   Union Personnel – benefit time payouts will follow the terms established in the applicable Collective Bargaining
          Agreement.
      •   Non-Union/Non-Elected Personnel – unused and accrued vacation time will be paid out. Accrued time is
          calculated on a prorated basis corresponding to the accrued days of service completed during the current
          anniversary year.
