9.15    VENDER REVIEW



9.16    ESCHEATING

PURPOSE

The purpose of this policy is to establish procedures for identifying, reporting, and remitting unclaimed property to the
Michigan Department of Treasury, ensuring Delta County remains in compliance with state law (MCL 567.221) and
protects the rights of the property owners.

SCOPE

This policy applies to all departments, offices, and agencies of Delta County government that hold intangible property,
including but not limited to:

    •   Uncashed checks (payroll, accounts payable, refunds)
    •   Court-Ordered deposits or restitution held in trust
    •   Overpayments and credit balances
    •   Fiduciary funds held for third-parties



As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                      Page 45 of 55


KEY DEADLINES and THRESHOLDS

To remain compliant with the Michigan Department of Treasury, the County shall adhere to the following statutory
timeline:

    •    Reporting Cut-Off Date: March 31 (Property is reviewed for “dormancy” as of this date)
    •    Annual Reporting Deadline: July 1 (Reports and funds must be remitted to the State)
    •    Dormancy Period: Generally, three (3) years for most property types (e.g. vendor checks, credits) but one (1)
         year for payroll wages.

PROCEDURES

    1.       Annual Review (April)
             On or before April 15 of each year, the Treasurer’s Office shall review all financial records to identify
             property that has reached its dormancy threshold as of March 31.
    2.       Due Diligence (April-May)
             For any unclaimed property valued at $25.00 or more, the County must send a written notice to the
             owner’s last known address.
                 • Timing: Notices must be sent no less than 60 days and no more than 365 days before filing the
                     report.
                 • Content: The letter must inform the owner that the County is in possession of the property and
                     that it will be turned over to the State if not claimed by a specified date.
    3.       Reporting and Remittances (by July 1)
                 • Electronic Filing: The County shall use the Michigan Treasury Online (MTO) portal to submit the
                     “Holder Report”.
                 • Remittance: Payment for the total value of reported unclaimed property shall be issued to “State
                     of Michigan-Unclaimed Property” and sent via the state-approved method.
                 • Negative Reporting: If no unclaimed property is identified for the cycle, the County will still file an
                     Attestation of Compliance (Form 4305) to maintain a clean audit trail.

RECORD RETENTION

The County shall retain records of unclaimed property reports, including owner names and last known addresses, for
ten (10) years following the date the property was reported, as required by MCL 567.252.

PENALTIES FOR NON-COMPLIANCE

Failure to report may result in the State of Michigan assessing:

    •    Interest: One percentage point above the adjusted prime rate.
    •    Civil Penalty: $100.00 per day (up to $5,000.00) or 25% of the property value for willful failure to report.

9.17     MICHIGAN UNIFORM CHART OF ACCOUNTS

PURPOSE

The purpose of this policy is to ensure that Delta County’s financial records are maintained, kept, and reported in
accordance with the Michigan Uniform Chart of Accounts (UCOA) as prescribed by the State Treasurer. This ensures
transparency, facilitates the annual audit process, and meets state reporting requirements. It also ensures Delta
County remains in full compliance with the Uniform Budgeting and Accounting Act (Public Act 2 of 1968) and MCL
141.421.

ADOPTION OF STATE STANDARDS



As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                         Page 46 of 55


Delta County formally adopts the most current edition of the Uniform Chart of Accounts for Local Units of Government
in Michigan, as published by the Michigan Department of Treasury.

    •   Annual Updates: The Administration Department shall review the UCOA annually (typically released/revised in
        January) to incorporate any new account numbers or activity definitions issued by the State.
    •   Suppression: This policy mandates the immediate discontinuation of any outdated or local-only account codes
        that conflict with the State Treasurer’s prescribed manual.

ACCOUNT STRUCTURE REQUIREMENTS

All County financial transactions must be coded using the mandatory three-tier structure defined by the State:

    •   Fund (3 digits): Identifies the specific fund (e.g. 101 for General Fund)
    •   Activity (3 digits): Identifies the functional area or department (e.g. 215 for County Clerk)
    •   Account (3 digits): Identifies the specific object of the transaction (e.g. 702 for salaries)

RESPONSIBILITIES

    •   Controller: The official charged with financial responsibility to ensure all general ledger entries conform to
        UCOA standards.
    •   Department Heads: When submitting budget requests or invoices, department heads must use the standardized
        activity and object codes designated for their specific operations.
    •   Internal Audits: The Administration Department will conduct periodic reviews of the General Ledger to identify
        and correct any misclassified accounts before the close of the fiscal year.

REPORTING COMPLIANCE

    •   Annual Financial Report (F-65): All data submitted to the State of Michigan in the annual financial report must
        be derived directly from the UCOA-compliant ledger.
    •   Searchability: In compliance with Treasury’s “Uniform Reporting Format”, all electronic audit files and
        financial statements must be searchable and formatted to allow for the verification of UCOA sub-control
        accounts.
    •   Deficit Elimination: Any fund found to be in a deficit position must be reported using the UCOA fund numbers
        to the Michigan Department of Treasury as part of a formal Deficit Elimination Plan, including Delta County
        Board of Commissioners Resolution.

CORRECTIVE ACTION

If a state audit or internal review identifies non-compliance with the UCOA:

    •   The County must rectify the account structure within ninety (90) business days of notification.
    •   If the County fails to maintain the UCOA, the State Treasurer has the authority under MCL 141.241(4) to
        perform the necessary services at the County’s expense.

9.18    THIRD-PARTY APPROPRIATIONS

PURPOSE

The purpose of this policy is to establish uniform procedures for the appropriation of County funds to external
organizations. This ensures that all disbursements comply with the Michigan Uniform Budgeting and Accounting Act and
that the County maintains rigorous oversight of public funds.

SCOPE



As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                     Page 47 of 55


This policy applies to any appropriation authorized by the Delta County Board of Commissioners to an entity that is not
a primary department or component unit of the County government.

APPROPRIATION REQUIREMENTS

No funds shall be disbursed to an outside organization unless the following conditions are met:

        1.       Public Purpose Doctrine: The organization must demonstrate that the funds will be used for a project
                 or service that provides a clear benefit to the citizens of Delta County.
        2.       Contractual Agreement: A valid Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) or Professional Services Contract
                 must be fully executed and on file with Administration, approved by the Board of Commissioners.
        3.       Proof of Status: Non-profit entities must provide proof of 501(c)(3) status and their most recent IRS
                 Form 990 or audited financial statement.

ADMINISTRATIVE PROCEDURES

Before any payment is processed by the Finance Department, the County Controller must certify the following:

        1.       Active Agreement: A current contract or MOU is in place that covers the specific fiscal year of the
                 appropriation.
        2.       Scope of Work: The contract clearly defines the deliverables, performance metrics, and the specific
                 public purpose served.
        3.       Indemnification: The agreement contains appropriate insurance and indemnification clauses protecting
                 Delta County.

Automated or recurring payments shall not be released until Administration confirms that the organization has met all
prior-year reporting requirements.

ANNUAL BOARD PRESENTATION REQUIREMENTS

To maintain transparency and evaluate the efficacy of the expenditure, each receiving organization is required to
present to the Board of Commissioners annually.

The presentation, at a minimum, must include:

        1.       Financial Accountability: A breakdown of exactly how the previous year’s County funds were spent.
        2.       Impact Metrics: Quantifiable data on services provided (i.e. number of residents served, miles of road
                 maintained, or economic impact generated).
        3.       Future Justification: A formal request for the upcoming year’s funding, outlining any changes in scope
                 or required increases.
        4.       Compliance Statement: Confirmation that the organization remains in good standing with state and
                 federal regulatory bodies.

COMPLIANCE AND OVERSIGHT

The Board of Commissioners reserves the right to audit the records of any organization receiving County funds as they
pertain to the specific appropriation. Failure to provide the annual presentation or maintain an active MOU/Contract
will result in the immediate suspension of funding.

9.19    FISCAL YEAR END CLOSING

PURPOSE




As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                      Page 48 of 55


To ensure that Delta County’s year-end financial statements are accurate, complete, and filed with the State of
Michigan in a timely manner. This policy establishes a standardized “Close Schedule” to facilitate a clean audit opinion
and avoid state penalties or deficit conditions. This policy incorporates the requirements of the Uniform Budgeting and
Accounting Act (PA 2 of 1968), the Michigan Department of Treasury’s Numbered Letters, and Generally Accepted
Accounting Principles (GAAP) for local governments.

YEAR-END CLOSING PROCEDURES (the “soft” and “hard” close)

    1.       Cut-Off Dates (October 1-October 31)
             a. Accounts Payable (Accruals): All goods and services received on or before September 30 must be
                 charged to the departing fiscal year. Invoices received in October for September services must be
                 accrued as “Accounts Payable”.
             b. Accounts Receivable: All revenue earned or grants due for activities occurring through September 30
                 must be recorded as “Accounts Receivable” or “Due from Other Governments”.
             c. Payroll Accrual: A pro-rated payroll accrual must be calculated for days worked in September that will
                 be paid in the October payroll cycle.

    2.       FINAL BUDGET AMENDMENTS (By September 30)
             a. In accordance with MCL 141.437, the Board of Commissioners must approve all necessary budget
                 amendments before the fiscal year ends on September 30. This is done during the last regular meeting
                 in September to allow the County Administrator or their designee to make such amendments as
                 required.
             b. Expenditures may not exceed appropriations at the departmental level. Any “over-expenditure”
                 discovered during the close must be corrected to avoid an audit finding.

    3.       INTER-FUND TRANSFERS & ADVANCES
             a. All due-to/due-from accounts between county funds (e.g. General Fund to Landfill Fund) must be
                 reconciled and, where possible, settled by September 30. Any remaining balances must be verified for
                 the audit trail.

STATUTORY REPORTING REQUIREMENTS

    1.       Annual Audit (the 6-Month Rule)
             a. Under MCL 141.425, Delta County must obtain an annual audit by a Certified Public Accountant.
                     i. Filing Deadline: The completed audit report and the Auditing Procedures Report (Form 496)
                        must be filed with the Michigan Department of Treasurer within 6 months of the fiscal year-end
                        (by March 31).
                    ii. Consequence: Failure to file by March 31 may result in the withholding of State Revenue
                        Sharing payments.

    2.       Annual Financial Report (F-65)
             a. The County must file the Local Unit Fiscal Report (F-65) electronically via the Treasury’s website by
                the same March 31 deadline. This report must align perfectly with the audited financial statements and
                the Uniform Chart of Accounts.

    3.       Deficit Elimination Plans
             a. If the year-end audit reveals a deficit in any individual fund (where expenditures/liabilities exceed
                 revenues/assets and fund balance), the County must:
                       i. Notify the State Treasurer within twelve (12) business days of discovery.
                      ii. Submit a formal Deficit Elimination Plan (compliant with Numbered Letter 2016-1) within 30
                          days of filing the audit.



As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                      Page 49 of 55


AUDIT PREPARDENESS CHECKLIST

To ensure the County is “audit-ready” by November, the Administration Department/Treasurer’s Department shall
compile the following:

    1.       Bank Reconciliations: Final September 30 statements for all County accounts.
    2.       Capital Asset Ledger: Update all equipment/property purchased or disposed of during the year (including
             depreciation).
    3.       Grant Schedule (SEFA): Complete list of federal/state grant expenditures for the Single Audit
    4.       Compensated Absences: Report of all vested sick and vacation time owed to employees as of September
             30.

9.20     GRANT AWARDS



9.21     JAIL COMMISSARY AND TRUST ACCOUNT



9.22     AIRPORT FUEL INVENTORY



9.23     AIRPORT FAA REQUIRED FORM SUBMISSIONS

PURPOSE

This policy is designed to ensure the airport remains self-sustaining while preventing revenue diversion. As a federally
obligated airport, Delta County must adhere to FAA Order 5190.6C (Airport Compliance Manual), which dictates how
airport revenue is handled and reported.

FINANCIAL POLICY FRAMEWORK

    1.        Revenue Use and Control: All revenue generated by the airport (fuel fees, hanger leases, landing fees,
              concessions) must be deposited into dedicated airport fund.
    2.        Prohibited Diversion: Airport funds cannot be transferred to the Delta County general fund for non-
              aviation projects.
    3.        Reasonable Rates: The Airport Manager must review lease agreements annually to ensure fair market
              value is charged to non-aeronautical tenants.

PROCUREMENT AND ASSET MANAGEMENT

    1.       FAA Competitive Bidding: For projects funded by the Airport Improvement Program (AIP), the airport must
             follow federal procurement standards (2 CFR Part 200), requiring sealed bids for contracts over $150,000.
             Airport Management will also follow County policy as written in 9.2 Purchasing and Procurement.
    2.       Inventory: An annual physical inventory of equipment purchased with federal funds must be conducted.

AUDIT REQUIREMENTS

Based on the Airport receiving federal grants, it must be included in Delta County’s annual Single Audit whenever
federal expenditures exceed $1,000,000 in a fiscal year.

MANDATORY FAA FORMS AND SUBMISSIONS




As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                       Page 50 of 55


The FAA monitors financial health through Certification Activity Tracking Systems (CATS). Failure to submit these forms
can result in the freezing of federal grant money. The Airport Manager is responsible for the submission of each of the
following forms within the deadline dictated by the FAA. Forms 5100-126 and 127 are due within 120 days of the fiscal
year end for the period reporting. Forms SF-425 is due within 30 days of quarter ending and Form 5100-100 is due
annually.

Form Number      Name of Form                    Description                                              Deadline

5100-126         Fin Gov Payment Rep             What the Airport pays the County for services            120 days FYE

5100-127         Op and Fin Summary              Operating revenue, expenses, non-op income               120 days FYE

SF-425           Federal Financial Rep           Status of AIP grant funds and expenditures               Quarterly

5100-110         Performance Report              Narrative on project progress for open grants            Annually

REQUIRED FORMS FOR NEW PROJECTS

When Delta County seeks new federal funding, the following must be submitted to the FAA:

         1.      FAA Form 5100-100: Application for Federal Assistance (Development Projects).
         2.      Exhibit A Property Map: A map showing the airport’s boundary and federally acquired land.
         3.      Grand Assurances: A signed agreement to comply with Part 139 specific federal obligations.

10       INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY



10.1     USER ACCOUNTS AND ACCESS RIGHTS

PURPOSE

This policy establishes the standards for creating, managing, and securing user accounts and access rights within the
Delta County network. It applies to all full-time, part-time, elected officials, and contracted employees, as well as any
third-party vendors with authorized network access.

USER ACCOUNT MANAGEMENT

To maintain a secure environment, account creation and maintenance must follow these guidelines:

         1.      Unique Identification: Every user must be assigned a unique User ID. Shared or Departmental accounts
                 are strictly prohibited unless authorized for specific service-level functions.
         2.      Onboarding and Provisioning: Access rights are granted based on the specific requirements of the
                 employee’s job description following the principle of least privilege.
         3.      Offboarding and Termination: Upon an employee’s separation from the county, all access rights must
                 be revoked immediately. Department heads are responsible for notifying IT in writing of personnel
                 changes within 24 hours.

ACCESS CONTROL AND RIGHTS

The county operates on the Principle of Least Privilege.

         1.      Role-Based Access: Access to sensitive folders, financial software, and resident data is restricted based
                 on departmental roles.




As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                       Page 51 of 55


        2.       Access Reviews: IT And Department Heads will conduct annual reviews of user permissions to ensure
                 they remain appropriate for current job duties.
        3.       Administrative Privileges: Administrative rights on individual workstations are restricted to prevent the
                 unauthorized installation of software or accidental system-wide changes.

PASSWORD AND AUTHENTICATION STANDARDS

Security begins with robust credential management.

        1.       Complexity Requirements: Passwords must meet minimum length and complexity standards based on
                 program requirements, industry best practices, and current regulations.
        2.       Multi-Factor Authentication: Where available, Multi-Factor Authentication must be enabled.
        3.       Confidentiality: Passwords must never be shared, written down in visible areas, or stored in
                 unencrypted digital files.
        4.       History Requirements: Passwords must not be reused.
        5.       Unique: Passwords must be unique for each login and not shared across systems.

SAFETY AND CONFIDENTIALITY PRACTICES

Employees are the first line of defense in protecting county data.

        1.       Lock-on-Leave: Users must lock their workstations whenever they step away from their desks to
                 prevent unauthorized physical access.
        2.       Remote Work: County business conducted off-site must use secure, county-approved methods. Public
                 Wi-Fi should never be used for accessing sensitive county records without a secure connection.
        3.       Data Handling: Confidential information regarding residents or county finances must not be transferred
                 to personal email accounts, unauthorized cloud storage, or unencrypted USB drives.

PROHIBITED ACTIVITIES

The following actions are considered a breach of security policy:

        1.       Attempting to bypass security controls or gain unauthorized access to data.
        2.       Using county resources for illegal activities or unauthorized commercial purposes.
        3.       Disclosing sensitive county information to unauthorized parties.

10.2    COUNTY INTERNET/EMAIL

PURPOSE

The purpose of this policy is to outline the acceptable use of internet and email services provided by Delta County. This
policy applies to all employees, contractors, and authorized users who access the internet via county-owned devices or
the county’s wired and wireless networks.

PROFESSIONAL USE AND PRODUCTIVITY

Internet and email access are provided primarily for the fulfillment of job-related responsibilities.

        1.       Business Priority: Use of the internet during work hours should be focused on County operations,
                 research, and communication with other governmental agencies or residents.
        2.       Incidental Personal Use: Brief and occasional personal use is permitted, provided it does not interfere
                 with job performance, consume significant system resources, or violate any other part of this policy or
                 other County policies.




As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                       Page 52 of 55


PROHIBITED ACTIVITIES

To maintain the integrity of the network and the reputation of the County, the following activities are strictly
prohibited:

        1.       Illegal Content: Accessing, downloading, or distributing material that is illegal, offensive, or
                 discriminatory.
        2.       Unauthorized Software: Downloading or installing software, browser extensions, or executable files
                 without prior approval from the IT Department.
        3.       Streaming and Bandwidth: Excessive use of non-work-related streaming services (video or music) that
                 may degrade network performance for other departments.
        4.       Bypassing Security: Attempting to circumvent County web filters or security protocols through the use
                 of proxies or VPNs.

SECURITY BEST PRACTICES

Users are responsible for practicing defensive browsing to protect the County from malware and phishing attacks.
Defensive Browsing looks like:

        1.       Verify Links: Before clicking on links in emails or on unfamiliar websites, hover over the link to verify
                 the destination URL.
        2.       Secure Sites: Ensure websites used for County business (especially those requiring login credentials or
                 financial data) use encryption (HTTPS).
        3.       Public Wi-Fi: When working remotely, County laptops should never connect to sensitive internal
                 systems via unsecured public Wi-Fi without using a County-approved VPN.

CONFIDENTIALITY AND DATA INTEGRITY

The internet is an open forum; users must treat all online communication with the same caution as public records.

        1.       Sensitive Information: Do not upload or transmit confidential resident data, internal financial
                 documents, or sensitive county records to unauthorized cloud storage or third-party websites.
        2.       Representing the County: Unless authorized, employees should not post opinions on social media or
                 public forums in a manner that suggest they are speaking officially for Delta County.

MONITORING AND COMPLIANCE

Users should have no expectation of privacy when using county-provided internet or email services. Delta County reserves
the right to monitor, log, and review internet traffic to ensure compliance with this policy. Email documents are subject
to the Michigan Freedom of Information Act to the same extent as, and with the same exemptions as, those applicable
to paper documents. Delta County reserves the right to inspect any email found in its system for its business activities
and to disclose email contents to appropriate personnel.

Misuse of internet and/or email privileges may result in the revocation of access and/or disciplinary action in accordance
with County policy.

10.3    EMPLOYEE CYBERSECURITY AWARENESS AND TRAINING

PURPOSE

The security of Delta County’s digital assets is a shared responsibility. Because human error is a leading cause of data
breaches, this policy establishes a continuous training program to protect county infrastructure, resident data, and
financial records. This policy applies to all employees, elected officials, and contractors with access to the County
network.



As Amended: 06/02/2026                                                                                         Page 53 of 55


IT DEPARTMENT AUTHORITY AND DISCRETION

To remain proactive against emerging threats, the IT Department is granted full discretion to manage the scope and
frequency of training.

        1. Curriculum Control: The IT Department will select training materials based on current global and local
           threat trends (i.e., specific phishing campaigns targeting Michigan municipalities).
        2. Mandatory Triggers: The IT Department has the authority to mandate immediate, unscheduled training
           modules for specific departments or the entire County in response to:
               a. A detected security incident
               b. The implementation of new software or hardware.
               c. Heightened threat alerts from state or federal agencies.

TRAINING STANDARDS AND REQUIREMENTS

To meet and exceed insurance carrier standards for proactive defense, the following training pillars are established:

        1. Initial Onboarding: All new hires must complete a foundational cybersecurity awareness module before
           being granted access to sensitive County systems.
        2. Annual Refresher: All users must complete a comprehensive annual security review covering password
           hygiene, social engineering, and data confidentiality.
        3. Specialized Training: Employees handling sensitive financial data, CJIS, or HIPAA-protected information
           may be required to complete additional, high-level security certifications at the discretion of the IT
           Department.

PHISHING SIMULATIONS

The IT Department will conduct unannounced phishing simulations at irregular intervals. These simulations are designed
to measure the County’s risk profile and provide “just-in-time” training to employees who interact with simulated
malicious content.

Employees who fail a simulation may be automatically enrolled in supplemental training to be completed within a
timeframe given by the IT Department when the training is required.

COMPLIANCE AND INSURANCE RECORDS

To ensure the County remains insurable and protected from liability:

        1. Automated Tracking: The IT Department will maintain digital records of all completed training, simulation
           results, and participation rates.
        2. Enforcement: Completion of mandated training is a condition of network access. Failure to complete
           training within the IT Department specified timeframe may result in temporary suspension of network
           privileges until the requirement is met.

BEST PRACTICES FOR EMPLOYEES

Employees are encouraged to report suspicious emails by contacting the IT Department directly. Prompt reporting is
considered a key success metric for this policy.

Security is not a once-a-year event; employees are expected to stay vigilant and treat cybersecurity as a core component
of their professional duties.

                                                                         Agenda Item B12
                                                                                     _________
                                                                         Date Added __________




                            DELTA COUNTY ADMINISTRATION OFFICE
                               310 LUDINGTON ST ESCANABA, MI 49829
                                          (906) 789-5100

                    DELTA COUNTY BOARD OF COMMISSIONERS
                            AGENDA ITEM REQUEST

Date:             9/10/26

Requestor Name:   Emily DeSalvo


Department:       Finance

Item Title:       General Budget Resolution


Meeting Date Requested and Attachments Included:      9/15/26        ☐

Explanation for Request:


The Board of Commissioners are requested to adopt the General Budget Resolution as
presented with amendments to reflect Board action taken at the 9-15-2026 meeting
regarding the FY27 Budget.


                                      RESOLUTION # 26-
                              GENERAL BUDGET RESOLUTION
       In accordance with the Budget Planning Committee recommendation:
        WE RESOLVE that there be levied and assessed upon the taxable properties, both real and
personal, in the several cities and townships of Delta County for the fiscal year 2026-2027, 4.9266
mils for a projected $7,909,255 to be used for General County Purposes.
       WE FURTHER RESOLVE that the County Administrator be hereby authorized to transfer
any remaining unspent budget balance in any fund, as of September 30, 2026, to the credit of the
General Fund.
        WE FURTHER RESOLVE that the County Board of Commissioners shall receive per diem
at the rate $20.00 per meeting up to two (2) hours, an additional $10.00 per hour after the initial
two (2) hours, up to $100.00 per day. Further, in no instance shall more than $100.00 per diem be
paid per day regardless of the number of meetings.
        The number of meetings qualifying for per diem compensation, held in Michigan’s Upper
Peninsula, shall be unlimited. However, meetings qualifying for per diem compensation, held at
locations not in Michigan’s Upper Peninsula, shall be limited and shall not exceed fifteen (15) days
per year.
        Mileage reimbursement for necessary travel shall be at the Board approved rate, according
to the Delta County Travel Reimbursement policy.
        WE FURTHER RESOLVE that the County Plat Board members shall receive per diem at
the rate of $20.00 per meeting up to two (2) hours, an additional $10.00 per hour after the initial
two (2) hours, up to $100.00 per day. Further, in no instance shall more than $100.00 per diem be
paid per day regardless of the number of meetings; plus, necessary travel at the Board approved
rate, according to the Delta County Travel Reimbursement policy.
        WE FURTHER RESOLVE that the Jury Commission members shall receive per diem at
the rate of $20.00 per meeting up to two (2) hours, an additional $10.00 per hour after the initial
two (2) hours, up to $100.00 per day. Further, in no instance shall more than $100.00 per diem be
paid per day regardless of the number of meetings; plus, necessary travel at the Board approved
rate, according to the Delta County Travel Reimbursement policy.
         WE FURTHER RESOLVE that the Delta County Board of Canvassers shall receive per
diem at the rate of $20.00 per meeting up to two (2) hours, an additional $10.00 per hour after the
initial two (2) hours, up to $100.00 per day. Further, in no instance shall more than $100.00 per
diem be paid per day regardless of the number of meetings; plus, necessary travel at the Board
approved rate, according to the Delta County Travel Reimbursement policy.
        WE FURTHER RESOLVE that the County Board of Commissioners be authorized to call
in lay help and that such persons may be authorized by action of the Board as provided by law to
receive the same rate of per diem and the necessary travel compensation per meeting as members


of the Board of Commissioners, such expenditure to be charged to the County Board of
Commissioner’s budget appropriation.
       WE FURTHER RESOLVE that meals for prisoners in the County Jail shall be provided
under the supervision of the Sheriff in accordance with the Board of Prisoners Budget Line Item
and the Food Service Contract as adopted.
        WE FURTHER RESOLVE that all County Departments must operate within these several
sums, as set forth in the 2026-2027 Delta County Budget as adopted September 15, 2026 and that
no overdraft whatsoever will be permitted, except upon recommendation of the Board.
Expenditures must be so planned that the appropriations will suffice for the entire fiscal year.
Travel vouchers must be presented monthly for payment and further all out-of-county travel,
except for Mental Health, Health Department, and Social Services personnel must have prior
approval by the County Board of Commissioners, the County Board’s Finance Chairperson, or the
County Administrator with Board ratification. All appropriations, including the Delta-Menominee
District Health Department and the Family Independence Agency, shall be made on a quarterly
basis except by approval of the County Board and upon written request by the department head.
All purchases must be in accordance with the attached Purchase Order Policy, unless otherwise
authorized by the Board.
       IN ORDER TO MAINTAIN A BALANCED BUDGET without additional reduction in
personnel all County Departments are to observe the following policies as approved by the Board
of Commissioners for the 2026-2027 budget year:
       1. No increase in staff without prior Board approval.
       2. No replacement hires without prior Board approval.
       3. Vacation relief expenditures, if any, must receive prior Board approval unless they fall
          within the scope of authority delegated to the County Administrator.
       4. Postage, duplication, and telephone expenses are to be kept to a minimum.
       WE FURTHER RESOLVE that the Jury fees per diem payment remain the same, but that
mileage be allowed at the Board approved rate, which shall be the State approved rate or the IRS
approved rate, whichever is less.
       WE FURTHER RESOLVE that the proposed Budget Resolution attached be adopted for
the 2026-2027 Budget.
       WE FURTHER RESOLVE that the annual salaries for the Elected Officials be set in the
attached Pay Schedule.


ELECTED POSITIONS                                          2026 SALARIES
COUNTY PROSECUTOR                                          $129971.00
SHERIFF                                                    $94,354.00
COUNTY CLERK/REGISTER OF DEEDS                             $81,284.00
COUNTY TREASURER                                           $78,809.00
DRAIN COMMISSIONER                                         $3,000.00

                    DELTA COUNTY BOARD OF COMMISSIONERS
                            AGENDA ITEM REQUEST

Date:             9/10/26

Requestor Name:   Emily DeSalvo


Department:       Finance

Item Title:       Budget Resolution


Meeting Date Requested and Attachments Included:      9/15/26        ☐

Explanation for Request:


The Board of Commissioners are requested to adopt the Budget Resolution as presented
with amendments to reflect Board action taken at the 9-15-2026 meeting regarding the
FY27 Budget.


                                  RESOLUTION #26-
                               BUDGET RESOLUTION


1. WHEREAS, the County Board has recognized the following as departments (cost centers)
   of the County’s General Fund.

2. WHEREAS, the County Board does not wish to change by this resolution the salaries of
   union and non-union positions as presently set by Board action and union contracts.

3. WHEREAS, the Board does not wish to change by adoption of this resolution its present
   policy regarding staffing levels.

4. WHEREAS, it is not the intent of the Board to change its present policy in regard to
   vacation relief help, if any, by adopting this resolution.

5. WHEREAS, the County Board recognized the County Administrator as Chief
   Administrative Officer.

6. WHEREAS, the County Board recognized the need of the following duly elected and
   appointed supervisors to properly administer their statutory duties as supervisory personnel
   of the County to wit:
           Circuit Court Judge                           Probate Court Judge
           District Court Judge                          County Clerk
           County Treasurer                              Sheriff
           Prosecutor                                    Drain Commissioner
           County Surveyor                               County Administrator
           Equalization Director                         Medical Examiner
           VSO Director                                  Airport Manager
           Director of Maintenance                       Parks Manager
           Dispatch Director                             IT Director
           Building and Zoning Administrator

7. WHEREAS, it is the responsibility of the County Board to fun necessary statutory
   obligations.


   8. WHEREAS, it is the County’s intent by adopting this resolution to comply with Public Act
      621 of 1978.

   9. WHEREAS, the County Board does not wish to change by adopting this resolution, its
      policy regarding out-of-county travel.

   10. WHEREAS, the County Board does not wish to change by adopting this resolution, its
       policy regarding equipment purchases.

   11. WHEREAS, the County Board does not wish to change by adopting this resolution, its
       present fringe benefit program.

   12. WHEREAS, the County Board does not wish to change by adopting this resolution, its
       policy regarding dues.

   13. WHEREAS, the County Board does not wish to change by adopting this resolution, its
       policy regarding its present line-item reporting of expenditures and revenues in relation to
       a line-item budget used as a supporting or supplemental document.

   14. WHEREAS, the County Administrator and/or Finance Committee will be granted authority
       to transfer amounts within and between departmental and/or contingency budgets in
       accordance with parameters as established by the Board as follows: “No more than $5,000
       or 10% of the department budget whichever is less.”

   15. WHEREAS, the Board wishes to mandate accountability with respect to the County
       Administrator’s and/or Finance Committee’s actions by requiring that they make a written
       presentation of transfers made or approved by them between meetings of the Board.

   16. WHEREAS, the Board does not wish to change its pre-employment policy.


NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that the County establish a departmental budget for the
General Fund in accordance with Public Act 621 of 1978. This adopted budget, as supplemented
by a detailed line-item budget, is to be adhered to by the County’s elected or appointed supervisors.
Further, the Board’s current position relative to salaries, staffing, out-of-county travel, vacation
relief, if any, equipment, fringes, dues, and pre-employment, is not altered by this resolution. And
further, that the County Administrator/Chief Financial Officer, acting as Chief Administrative
Officer, and/or the Finance Committee, be allowed to make transfers within and between
departmental and/or contingency budgets in accordance with parameters as established by the
Board as follows: “No more than $5,000 or 10% of the department budget whichever is less.”

We further resolve that the General Appropriations Act encompass the attached budgets for the
other funds specified and that the budgetary authority for said funds shall be consistent with the
State Statute based on the total amount specified by fund.


Adopted: 9-15-26

I, Nancy J. Przewrocki, Delta County Clerk and Clerk of the Delta County Board of
Commissioners do hereby certify this to be a true and exact copy from the minutes of the regular
meeting of the Delta County Board of Commissioners held on September 15, 2026.

I, Nancy J. Przewrocki, Delta County Clerk do hereby set my hand and seal this 15th day of
September, 2026.

Item Title:       Human Services Agency Resolution

Explanation for Request:


The Board of Commissioners are requested to adopt the Human Services Agency
Resolution as presented.


                                     RESOLUTION # 26-
                   HUMAN SERVICES AGENCY BOARD RESOLUTION


        RESOLVED, THAT WHEREAS, Act 380 of the Public Act of 1939, as amended provides
that the County Board of Commissioners shall fix the salary of the members of the County Human
Services Agency Board.
        THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that the members and advisory members of the Delta
County Human Services Agency Board shall receive the same rate per diem as members of the
General Resolution for a maximum of twenty-four (24) meetings per year, plus their necessary
travel expenses, according to the Delta County Travel Reimbursement Policy.

                            AGENDA ITEM REQUEST

Date:             9/10/26

Requestor Name:   Emily DeSalvo


Department:       Finance

Item Title:       Road Commission Resolution


Meeting Date Requested and Attachments Included:     9/15/26        ☐

Explanation for Request:


The Board of Commissioners are requested to adopt the Road Commission Resolution as
presented.


                                      RESOLUTION # 26-
                            ROAD COMMISSION RESOLUTION


        WE RESOLVE that the per diem of the County Road Commissioners be fixed at the same
rate as that of the Delta County Board of Commissioners, plus necessary mileage at the Board
approved rate, according to the Delta County Travel Reimbursement policy.
       WE FURTHER RESOLVE that the per diem of the members of the County Board of Road
Commissioners shall be paid as follows: One full meeting of assignment or any meeting or
assignment $20.00 per meeting up to two (2) hours, an additional $10.00 per hour after the initial
two (2) hours, up to $100.00 per day. Further, in no instance shall more than $100.00 per diem be
paid per day regardless of the number of meetings. The rate of mileage shall be at the Board
approved rate, according to the Delta County Travel Reimbursement Policy.
        WE FURTHER RESOLVE that the salary of the County Board of Road Commissioners be
set up to $525.00 per month as determined by official motion of the County Board of Road
Commissioners at a regular Road Commission meeting specifying salary amounts, which will be
based upon the County Board of Road Commissioners determination of Road Commission’s
financial condition to support the stated salary.
       WE FURTHER RESOLVE That the County Board of Road Commissioners be allowed
optional medical insurance coverage under the Road Commission Group Policy. The total of the
medical premium 100%, is to be paid by the individual members. All insurance coverage must
follow applicable COBRA Law.
